(3 D/C detection logic) CMP sensor and signal rotor CKP sensor and sensor plate Timing chain Timing chain tensioner Timing chain tensioner adjuster VVT actuator ECM DTC Confirmation Procedure If you do not select a suitable route and take proper precautions when performing a road test, an accident can occur. Select a level road with no traffic to minimize the risk of accident. Perform the test extremely carefully. Perform the road test with 2 persons, a driver and a tester. Limit the driving speed permissible in road conditions and regulations. Warm up engine and repeat acceleration and deceleration between 30 and 80 km/h (19 and 50 mile/h) more than 10 times. DTC Troubleshooting Was “Engine and Emission Control System Check” performed? Go to Step 2. Go to Engine and Emission Control System Check:K14C . CMP sensor, signal rotor and its circuit check Check CMP sensor, signal rotor and its circuit related to DTC according to Step 2 – 5 under DTC P0340:K14C . Is check result OK? Go to Step 3. Repair or replace defective part. CKP sensor, sensor plate and its circuit check Check CKP sensor, sensor plate and its circuit related to DTC according to Step 2 – 5 under DTC P0335 / P0336:K14C . Is check result OK? Go to Step 4. Repair or replace defective part. Engine mechanical system check Remove timing chain cover. Check the following items. Timing chain, timing chain tensioner and timing chain tensioner adjuster: VVT actuator: Is check result OK? Go to Step 5. Repair or replace defective part. Troubleshooting of other DTC(s) at the same time Is other DTC(s) detected together? Go to troubleshooting for applicable DTC. Replace ECM and recheck for DTC.

- AENLSB0A1114032 -> ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A1114032.xml | DTC P0137 / P0138 / P0140:K14C
- AENLSB0A1114029 -> ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A1114029.xml | DTC P0131 / P0132:K14C
Excerpt:
%sdatacharaents; ]> DTC P0420 DTC Detecting Condition and Trouble Area DTC detecting condition Trouble area P0420: Catalyst System Efficiency Below Threshold (Bank1) The calculated degradation index is below specified value. (3 D/C detection logic) Exhaust system HO2S-1 and/or its circuit HO2S-2 and/or its circuit TWC ECM Circuit Diagram [A]: ECM connector (View: [a]) 2. TWC 4. ECM 1. HO2S-1 3. HO2S-2 DTC Confirmation Procedure If you do not select a suitable route and take proper precautions when performing a road test, an accident can occur. Select a level road with no traffic to minimize the risk of accident. Perform the test extremely carefully. Perform the road test with 2 persons, a driver and a tester. Limit the driving speed permissible in road conditions and regulations. Check that the following conditions are met in this “DTC Confirmation Procedure”. A/F feedback (closed loop) control is activated. ECT at engine start > –9 °C (16 °F) Check that the following DTCs are not detected in this “DTC Confirmation Procedure”. HO2S-1 (P0131, P0132, P0133 and P0134) HO2S-2 (P0137, P0138, P0140 and P2271) Warm up engine to normal operating temperature. Drive vehicle at 60 to 100 km/h (37 to 62 mile/h) constantly for 10 min. or more at 6th gear (M/T model) or D range (A/T model). (Keep throttle valve opening constant in this step.) Check whether catalyst readiness / monitoring test has completed or not using scan tool. If catalyst readiness / monitoring test has not completed, check vehicle conditions (environmental) and repeat Step 2) – 3). DTC Troubleshooting Was “Engine and Emission Control System Check” performed? Go to Step 2. Go to Engine and Emission Control System Check:K14C . DTC check Check DTC. Is there DTC(s) detected other than P0420? Go to troubleshooting for applicable DTC. Go to Step 3. Exhaust system check Check exhaust system for leakage, damage and looseness. Is check result OK? Go to Step 4. Repair or replace defective parts. HO2S-2 circuit check Check HO2S-2 signal and ground circuits according to Step 3 under DTC P0137 / P0138 / P0140:K14C . Is check result OK? Go to Step 5. Repair or replace defective wire harness. HO2S-1 signal circuit check

## AENLSB0A1416047 - Piston, Piston Ring, Connecting Rod and Cylinder Removal and Installation
Path: ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A1416047.xml
Scopes: bookmarks: 1 - Engine > 1D - Engine Mechanical > Repair Instructions > K14C

- AENLSB0A1416046 -> ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A1416046.xml | Piston, Piston Ring, Connecting Rod and Cylinder Components:K14C
- AENLSB0A1416048 -> ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A1416048.xml | Piston, Piston Ring, Connecting Rod and Cylinder Disassembly and Reassembly:K14C
- AENLSB0A1416050 -> ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A1416050.xml | Piston Pin and Connecting Rod Inspection:K14C
Excerpt:
%sdatacharaents; ]> Piston, Piston Ring, Connecting Rod and Cylinder Removal and Installation Piston, Piston Ring, Connecting Rod and Cylinder Components:K14C If any of the parts listed below is reinstalled into a position different from the original position, engine problems could occur. If these parts are to be reused, keep them in numerical order so that they can be reinstalled in their original positions. Connecting rods Connecting rod bearing caps Connecting rod bearings Pistons Removal Remove engine assembly from vehicle. Remove cylinder head. Mark cylinder numbers on all pistons, connecting rods and connecting rod bearing caps using silver pencil or quick drying paint. Remove connecting rod bearing caps. Remove carbon deposits from top of cylinder bore before removing piston from cylinder. Push piston and connecting rod assembly out from the top of cylinder bore. Remove connecting rod bearings from connecting rod and connecting rod bearing cap, if necessary. Installation Piston, Piston Ring, Connecting Rod and Cylinder Disassembly and Reassembly:K14C The engine will seize up if it is operated when the parts listed below are not properly lubricated. Lubricate all the friction surfaces of these parts with engine oil before installing them. Crankshaft pins Connecting rod bearings Pistons Piston rings Cylinder walls Install connecting rod bearing to connecting rod bearing cap and connecting rod as follows. Check mating surfaces of bearing backside and corresponding connecting rod are clean and dry. Fit tab (1) of connecting rod bearing to groove (2) of connecting rod and bearing cap. Press bearing end (3) until it becomes flush with connecting rod or connecting rod bearing cap mating surface. If oil is present between the bearing backside and connecting rod surface, the bearing could heat up to very high temperature, resulting in seizure. Thoroughly wipe off any trace of oil that is present between the bearing backside and connecting rod surface. Apply engine oil to pistons, piston rings, cylinder walls, connecting rod bearings and crankshaft pins. When inserting piston and connecting rod assembly into cylinder bore, point front mark (1) on piston head to cra

%sdatacharaents; ]> Main Bearing, Crankshaft and Cylinder Block Removal and Installation Main Bearing, Crankshaft and Cylinder Block Components:K14C If any of the parts listed below is reinstalled into a position different from the original position, engine problems could occur. If these parts are to be reused, keep them in numerical order so that they can be reinstalled in their original positions. Main bearings Thrust bearings For ENG A-STOP model, main bearing No.1 upper half (1) and main bearing No.2 – 5 upper halves (2) have holes (3) in different positions. Distinguish them for installation. Removal Remove engine assembly from vehicle. Remove flywheel (M/T model) or drive plate (A/T model). Remove pistons and connecting rods. Remove oil seal housing (1). Remove rear oil seal from oil seal housing. Remove CKP sensor. Remove main bearing cap bolts in numerical order (“1 – “10”) evenly and gradually. Remove main bearing caps. Remove crankshaft from cylinder block. Remove sensor plate from crankshaft, if necessary. Use T-30 security Torx ® to loosen sensor plate screws. Remove piston cooling jets (1), if necessary. Remove oil cooler, if necessary. Remove generator, if necessary. Remove water pump, if necessary. Remove PCV valve, if necessary. Remove breather plate (1), if necessary. Remove knock sensor, if necessary. Remove oil temperature sensor, if necessary. Installation Crankshaft Inspection:K14C Main Bearing Inspection:K14C Sensor Plate Inspection:K14C Flywheel / Drive Plate Inspection:K14C Cylinder Block Inspection:K14C The engine will seize up if it is operated when the parts listed below are not properly lubricated. Lubricate all the friction surfaces of these parts with engine oil before installing them. Crankshaft journals Main bearings (Sliding surface) Thrust bearings Using main bearing cap bolts that are deformed exceeding the service limit could lead to engine problems. When main bearing cap bolts are reused, check that main bearing cap bolts are not deformed exceeding the service limit referring to “Main Bearing Cap Bolt” under Main Bearing Inspection:K14C . Install new oil temperature sensor, if removed. Install knock sensor, if removed. Instal

Go to Step 4. Brake light switch power supply circuit check With ignition “OFF”, disconnect brake light switch connector. Check for proper terminal connection to brake light switch connector. If connection is OK, set ignition “ON”. Check that voltage between “A1” and “C1” circuits are battery voltage. Is check result OK? Go to Step 6. Go to Step 5. Brake light switch ground circuit check Check that voltage between “A1” circuit and ground is battery voltage. Is check result OK? Repair or replace “C1” circuit. Repair or replace “A1” circuit. Brake light drive circuit check With ignition “OFF”, disconnect “E09” connector. Check for proper terminal connection to “E09” connector. If connection is OK, check the following points. Resistance of “B1” circuit: less than 1 Ω Resistance between “B1” circuit and ground: infinity Resistance between “B1” circuit terminal and each terminal at brake light switch connector: infinity Is check result OK? Go to Step 7. Repair or replace defective wire harness. Brake light signal circuit check Check the following points. Resistance of “B2” circuit: less than 1 Ω Resistance between “B2” circuit terminal and each terminal at brake light switch connector: in

Sensor calibration Calibrate all sensors. Perform DTC Check . Is DTC C1074, C1075, C1076 and/or C1078 still detected? Adjustment Differential side bearing preload After applying ATF to differential assembly (1), fit it to transaxle case. Install torque converter housing to transaxle case and tighten torque converter housing bolts in numerical order (“1” – “22”) to specified torque. There are three kinds of bolts (bolts A (1), B (2) and C (3)) for fixing torque converter housing. Torque converter housing bolt length Bolt Length Pieces A (1) 30 mm (1.18 in.) 4 B (2) 35 mm (1.38 in.) 16 C (3) 39 mm (1.54 in.) 2 Torque converter housing bolt a 29 3.0 21.5 Measure bearing preload using special tool. (A) 09928–06070 Differential side bearing preload measured as starting torque In case of new bearing: 0.06 – 1.85 N·m (0.61 – 18.86 kgf-cm, 0.05 – 1.40 lbf-ft) In case of reused bearing: 0.03 – 0.93 N·m (0.31 – 9.48 kgf-cm, 0.03 – 0.70 lbf-ft) If check result is not as specified, select shim with suitable thickness from the following table and replace it. Then adjust differential side bearing preload within specification. Record measured differential side bearing, because it is necessary to adjust countershaft bearing preload. Driver air bag (inflator) module circuit Contact coil assembly SDM Circuit Diagram [A]: 2P connector with 2 pins [F]: “G12” connector (View: [a]) 3. SDM [B]: 4P connector with 2 pins A1: Driver air bag (inflator) module deployment signal output circuit 4. Shorting bar [C]: “S121” connector (View: [a]) A2: Driver air bag (inflator) module deployment signal ground circuit 5. Instrument panel harness [D]: “G347” connector (View: [a]) 1. Driver air bag (inflator) module [E]: “G346” connector (View: [a]) 2. Contact coil assembly DTC Troubleshooting Was “Air Bag Diagnostic System Check” performed? Go to Step 2. Go to Air Bag Diagnostic System Check . Driver air bag (inflator) module circuit check Check that ignition is “OFF”. Disconnect “G346” or “G347” connector. Disconnect “G12” connector. Release shorting bar in SDM connector. Set ignition “ON”. Check the following point. Voltage between each of “A1” and “A2” circuits and ground: 0 V 09932–76010 Is check result OK? Go to Step 3. Replace instrument panel harness. Contact coil circuit check Check that ignition is “OFF”. Connect “G346” or “G347” connector. Remove driver air bag (inflator) module from steering wheel. Release shorting bar in SDM connector. Set ignition “ON”. Check the following point. Voltage between each of “A1” and “A2” circuits and ground: 0 V 09932–76010 Is check result OK? Replace SDM and recheck DTC. Replace contact coil assembly. Upon completion of inspection and repair work, perform the following items. Reconnect all air bag system components and check that all components are properly mounted. Clear DTCs, referring to DTC Clearance if any DTC is detected. Repeat Air Bag Diagnostic System Check to check that the trouble has been solved.

%sdatacharaents; ]> DTC C1640 DTC Detecting Condition and Trouble Area DTC detecting condition Trouble area C1640: RBS Control Temporary Inhibition RBS control module detects this DTC if any one of the following conditions is met. Poor visibility of millimeter wave radar. High temperature in RBS control module. Power supply for RBS control module is not between 10.0 and 15.5 V for 2 seconds continuously. RBS control module received temporary stop signal from ESP ® control module. RBS control module received invalid outside air temperature sensor signal from BCM. Millimeter wave radar surface RBS control module power supply / ground circuit Charging system Outside air temperature sensor RBS control module Circuit Diagram [A]: RBS control module connector (View: [a]) 1. RBS control module 4. “IG1 SIG2” fuse A1: RBS control module power supply circuit 2. CAN driver 5. Each control module K14C model: CAN Communication System Circuit Diagram:CAN (K14C Model) K14D model: CAN Communication System Circuit Diagram:CAN (K14D Model) B1: RBS control module ground circuit 3. IG11 relay System Description When RBS control module judges that the system impairs the original performance, RBS control module suspends adaptive cruise control, and outputs DTC C1640. However, when the cause of the suspension is eliminated, RBS control module turns off adaptive cruise control warning light, clears the DTC and resumes the system function automatically. DTC Confirmation Procedure Set ignition “ON”. DTC Troubleshooting DTC C1640 can be detected as a current DTC but it is not stored as a history DTC. When any malfunction is detected on each parameter in the following table, “NG” indication is maintained. Therefore, after resuming the system to be normal, the factor of the malfunction can be checked in “Data List” of SUZUKI scan tool. The “NG” indication is maintained until the DTC is cleared. Check each parameter in the table below using SUZUKI scan tool. Diagnose the defective parameter according to diagnosis flow. If diagnosis flow does not correspond to the parameter, perform Adaptive Cruise Control System Check:Adaptive Cruise Control . Parameter Value Diagnosis flow Radar Visibility

Lighting and turn signal switch DTC Confirmation Procedure If you do not select a suitable route and take proper precautions when performing a road test, an accident can occur. Select a level road with no traffic to minimize the risk of accident. Perform the test extremely carefully. Perform the road test using 2 persons, a driver and a tester. Steering angle sensor check Check steering angle sensor. Is check result OK? Check for intermittent trouble. Replace lighting and turn signal switch and recheck DTC.

ESP ® control module C1654: Yaw Rate Sensor Circuit RBS control module detects invalid yaw rate signal from ESP ® control module. DTC Confirmation Procedure If you do not select a suitable route and take proper precautions when performing a road test, an accident can occur. Select a level road with no traffic to minimize the risk of accident. Perform the test extremely carefully. Perform the road test using 2 persons, a driver and a tester. Set ignition “ON”. Yaw rate sensor check Connect SUZUKI scan tool to DLC when ignition is “OFF”. Set ignition “ON”, and select “Data List” mode in “ESP ® control module” on SUZUKI scan tool. Check “Yaw Rate Sensor” displayed on SUZUKI scan tool. Is check result OK? Check for intermittent trouble. Replace ESP ® control module and recheck DTC.

Perform DTC Confirmation Procedure and then recheck DTC. Is DTC B1A2C, B1A2D, B1A2E or B1A2F still detected? Go to Step 4. Check for intermittent trouble. DTC check Replace tire pressure sensor for detected DTC. Clear DTC. Perform DTC Confirmation Procedure and then recheck DTC. Is DTC B1A2C, B1A2D, B1A2E or B1A2F still detected? Replace TPMS control module and recheck DTC. End.

DTC Troubleshooting This DTC might be detected when battery is run down or the voltage is very low. Battery voltage check Check that battery terminal voltage is 12 V or more. Is check result OK? Go to Step 2. Check battery and generator. K14C model Battery Inspection:K14C Generator Test:K14C K14D model Battery Inspection:K14D Generator Test:K14D DTC check Clear DTC. Perform DTC Confirmation Procedure and then recheck DTC. Is current DTC B115E detected? Go to Step 3. End. (Intermittent trouble.) Steering lock unit power supply circuit check Disconnect negative (–) cable at battery. Disconnect steering lock unit and “G06” connectors. Check for proper terminal connection to steering lock unit and “G06” connectors. If connections are OK, check the following points. Resistance between “A2” circuit and ground: infinity Resistance between “A2” circuit terminal and each terminal at “G06” connector: infinity Is check result OK? Replace BCM and recheck DTC. Repair or replace defective wire harness.

Path: ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A1416036.xml
Scopes: bookmarks: 1 - Engine > 1D - Engine Mechanical > Repair Instructions > K14C
Top outgoing references:
- AENLSB0A1416035 -> ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A1416035.xml | Camshaft Components:K14C
- AENLSB0A1416037 -> ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A1416037.xml | Camshaft and VVT Actuator Inspection:K14C
Excerpt:
%sdatacharaents; ]> Camshaft Removal and Installation Camshaft Components:K14C Removal Remove timing chain. Remove vacuum pump. Remove CMP sensor, if necessary. Remove high pressure fuel pump. Remove thermostat case (1) with thermostat and O-rings (2) from cylinder head. With hexagonal section of intake camshaft held stationary with spanner or adjustable wrench (1), remove VVT actuator bolt (2) and remove VVT actuator (3). Apply the same procedure to remove exhaust camshaft timing sprocket (4). Remove dowel pins (1) from intake camshaft and exhaust camshaft. Loosen camshaft housing bolts No.1, No.2 and camshaft intake rear housing bolts in numerical order (“1” – “23”) evenly and gradually. Remove camshaft front upper housing (1), camshaft housings (2) and camshaft intake rear housing (3). Remove intake camshaft (4) and exhaust camshaft (5). Remove camshaft front lower housing (6). Installation Camshaft and VVT Actuator Inspection:K14C Before installing camshafts to the cylinder head, turn crankshaft counterclockwise and position the key (1) within an angle of 30° – 90° from TDC as shown in figure and keep the valves from coming in contact with the piston. “a”: 30° “b”: 90° Check that all dowel pins are installed to cylinder head and camshaft front lower housing. Fit camshaft front lower housing (1) into cylinder head. Apply engine oil to bearing portions of camshaft front lower housing and cylinder head. Set intake camshaft (2) and exhaust camshaft (3) on cylinder head, and apply engine oil to sliding surfaces. Set camshaft housings No.2 – 5 (1) to specified places as indicated by alphabet (2), number (3) and arrow mark (4) on each camshaft housing. 2. I: Intake camshaft housing E: Exhaust camshaft housing 3. Position from timing chain side 4. Point arrow to timing chain side Clean mating surface of camshaft intake rear housing and cylinder head. Apply sealant “A” to mating surface of camshaft intake rear housing as shown in figure. “A” Sealant 99000–31260 SUZUKI Bond No.1217G Sealant bead size for camshaft intake rear housing Width “a”: 3 mm (0.12 in.) Height “b”: 2 mm (0.08 in.) Fit camshaft intake rear housing (1) on cylinder head and tighten the housing bolt

Excerpt:
%sdatacharaents; ]> Valve and Cylinder Head Removal and Installation Valve and Cylinder Head Components:K14C Removal Remove timing chain. Remove spark plugs. Remove intake and exhaust camshafts. Remove water inlet pipe. Disconnect PCV valve hose (1). Remove catalytic converter. Remove turbocharger. Remove valve rocker arms (1) from cylinder head. Remove valve lash adjusters (1) from cylinder head. Immerse removed valve lash adjusters in clean engine oil and keep them as they are until their reinstallation to prevent entering of air in the adjusters. Remove cylinder head bolts in numerical order (“1” – “10”) evenly and gradually. Check all around cylinder head for any other parts required to be removed or disconnected and remove or disconnect them if any. Remove cylinder head (1) from cylinder block. Remove dowel pins (2) from cylinder block, if necessary. Remove cylinder head gasket (3) from cylinder block. Remove the following components from cylinder head, if necessary. Intake manifold: Fuel delivery pipe and fuel injectors: Installation Valve and Cylinder Head Disassembly and Reassembly:K14C Clean mating surfaces of cylinder head and cylinder block. Remove oil, old gasket and dust from mating surfaces. Install dowel pins (1) to cylinder block, if removed. Set new cylinder head gasket (2) to cylinder block. Install cylinder head to cylinder block according to the following procedure. Before installing the cylinder head to the cylinder block, turn crankshaft counterclockwise and position the key (1) within an angle of 30° – 90° from TDC as shown in figure and keep the valves from coming in contact with the piston. “a”: 30° “b”: 90° If cylinder head bolt is reused, check bolt for deformation. Apply engine oil to thread for 4 mm (0.15 in.) from lower end of cylinder head bolts. Tighten cylinder head bolts to 15 N·m (1.5 kgf-m, 11.0 lbf-ft) in numerical order (“1” – “10”) as shown using a 12-point socket wrench. In the same manner as in Step b), tighten them to 30 N·m (3.1 kgf-m, 22.5 lbf-ft). Retighten all cylinder head bolts 70° in numerical order as shown in figure. Retighten all cylinder head bolts 65° in numerical order as shown in figure. Cylinder head bolt*

## AENLSB0A1A26005 - ISG Removal and Installation
Path: ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A1A26005.xml
Scopes: unscoped
Top outgoing references:

Excerpt:
%sdatacharaents; ]> ISG Removal and Installation Servicing SHVS system involves a danger of electric shock. For precaution, detect the terminal voltage and use insulation tool and gloves. The ISG consists of precision parts. Without taking the following precautions, it may lose its original performance. Do not disassemble the ISG. Do not drop or expose the ISG to large shock. Before performing the following procedure, observe Precautions for Handling ISG:K14D . Removal Set ignition “OFF”, and keep ignition “OFF” for 3 minutes. Disconnect negative (–) cable at battery. Hoist vehicle and remove engine undercover and right side engine under side cover. Remove accessory drive belt. Remove radiator cooling fan assembly. With A/C hoses connected, detach A/C compressor from cylinder block, and disconnect magnet clutch lead wire connector. When A/C compressor is detached, the A/C hoses could be damaged receiving weight of the compressor. Suspend the removed A/C compressor to the vehicle body while the compressor is detached. Remove accessory drive belt lower tensioner. Detach harness clamps (1) and ISG connector (2). Remove terminal cover (1), and check terminal voltage between “48V+” (2) and “48V–” (3) is lower than 5V. If the voltage is still higher than 5V, check it again in 15 minutes. Disconnect “48V–” terminal wire. Disconnect “48V+” terminal wire. Remove ISG bolts (1). Remove ISG (2) from vehicle. Remove ISG bracket (1), if necessary. Remove harness bracket (1) and suction hose stay (2), if necessary. Installation ISG Inspection:K14D Install harness bracket and suction hose stay as follows, if removed. Touch bracket stopper (1) to ISG and tighten bolt (2) to specified torque. Harness bracket bolt a 10 1.0 7.5 Set suction hose stay to its original position and tighten bolt (3) securely. Tighten ISG bracket bolts (1), if removed. ISG bracket bolt a 55 5.6 40.5 2. ISG bracket Install ISG (1) as follows. Put ISG on cylinder block and tighten new ISG bolts (2) temporarily. Tighten ISG bolts to 30 N·m (3.1 kgf-m, 22.5 lbf-ft) and check that bushings (3) in ISG bracket are in contact with ISG bracket and cylinder block, and then loosen the bolts. Tighten ISG bolts in nu

Excerpt:
%sdatacharaents; ]> EBD Warning Light (Brake Warning Light) Comes ON Steady System Description EBD warning light (brake warning light) is controlled by ESP ® control module and BCM through light driver module in combination meter. If ESP ® system is in good condition, ESP ® control module turns ON EBD warning light (brake warning light) when ignition is set “ON”, keeps it ON for 2 seconds and then turns it OFF. EBD warning light (brake warning light) is turned ON continuously in the following conditions. EBD control is deactivated by ESP ® system failure. Parking brake lever is pulled up. Brake fluid level is lower than minimum level. The information of parking brake switch and brake fluid level is transmitted from BCM to light driver module in combination meter through CAN communication line. Also, the warning light is turned ON continuously by light driver module when the connector of ESP ® control module is disconnected. Troubleshooting ESP® control module connector check Check for proper connection to ESP ® control module connector. Is connection in good condition? Go to Step 2. Connect connector securely. Parking brake and brake fluid level check Check the following points. Parking brake is completely released. Brake fluid level is above minimum level. Is check result OK? Go to Step 3. Release parking brake and check brake fluid level. ABS warning light operation check Set ignition “ON”. Does ABS warning light stay on? Perform ABS Warning Light Comes ON Steady . Go to Step 4. Parking brake switch signal circuit and brake fluid level switch signal circuit check Check parking brake switch signal circuit and brake fluid level switch signal circuit. Is check result OK? Go to Step 5. Repair or replace defective part. CAN communication circuit check Check that ignition is “OFF”. Connect scan tool to DLC. Set ignition “ON”. Perform DTC Check . Is DTC U0073 detected? K14C model: Go to Troubleshooting for Communication Bus Off:CAN (K14C Model) . K14D model: Go to Troubleshooting for Communication Bus Off:CAN (K14D Model) . Go to Step 6. DTC check for BCM Check BCM for DTC. Is DTC U0073 detected? K14C model: Go to Troubleshooting for Communication Bus Off:CAN (K14C M

ESP ® control module Actuator bracket C1017: Lateral G Sensor Circuit Performance Lateral G sensor signal is out of specified range. ## AENLSB0A5306018 - Clutch Cover and Clutch Disc Removal and Installation
Path: ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A5306018.xml
Scopes: bookmarks: 5 - Transmission / Transaxle > 5C - Clutch > Repair Instructions
Top outgoing references:
- AENLSB0A5306017 -> ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A5306017.xml | Clutch Cover and Clutch Disc Components
- AENLSB0A5306019 -> ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A5306019.xml | Clutch Cover and Clutch Disc Inspection
Excerpt:
%sdatacharaents; ]> Clutch Cover and Clutch Disc Removal and Installation Clutch Cover and Clutch Disc Components Removal Dismount manual transaxle. Hold flywheel with special tool and remove clutch cover (1) and clutch disc. (A) 09924–17811 Installation Clutch Cover and Clutch Disc Inspection Before assembling, check the following items. Contact surfaces of flywheel, pressure plate and clutch disk are clean and dry. Old grease and dust are removed from input shaft and clutch disk hub spline. Hold flywheel with special tool (A). Align clutch disc with crankshaft center using special tool (B) and install clutch cover (1). Install and tighten clutch cover bolts using the following procedure: Tighten 3 bolts (3) by hand. Tighten other bolts (4) evenly and gradually to 5.0 N·m (0.51 kgf-m, 4.0 lbf-ft). Fully tighten all bolts evenly and gradually to specified torque in numerical order (“1” – “6”) starting from bolt nearby strut plate rivet (2). For centering of clutch disc spline, tighten clutch cover bolts while pushing clutch disc with special tool. (A) 09924–17811 (B) 09923–36340 Clutch cover bolt* a 5.0 N·m → 23 N·m (0.51 kgf-m → 2.3 kgf-m, 4.0 lbf-ft → 17.0 lbf -ft) Clutch cover bolt b 23 2.3 17.0 Slightly apply grease to input shaft (1), and then join manual transaxle with engine. When a large quantity of grease is applied, grease may be scattered to clutch disc. When grease is applied, reduce quantity as much as possible. When replacing clutch disc with new one, it is not necessary to apply grease to input shaft. When inserting transaxle input shaft to clutch disc, turn crankshaft little by little to match the splines. “A” Grease 99000–25210 SUZUKI Super Grease I

%sdatacharaents; ]> DTC C1153 / C1155 DTC Detecting Condition and Trouble Area DTC detecting condition Trouble area C1153: Power Supply Voltage Too Low or Too High Measured voltage of fail-safe relay contact is lower than 9 V even if engine speed is more than 600 rpm and fail-safe relay ON is commanded, or voltage at fail-safe relay contact is higher than 19.2 V. Charging system P/S control module (steering column assembly) C1155: P/S Control Module Internal Failure P/S control module internal failure. Circuit Diagram [A]: P/S control module connector “E61” (View [a]) 1. P/S control module / motor assembly 4. “P/S” fuse A1: P/S control module power supply circuit 2. CPU 5. To continuous power supply A2: P/S control module ground circuit 3. Relay box DTC Confirmation Procedure Run engine at idle speed for more than 5 seconds. DTC Troubleshooting Was “P/S System Check” performed? Go to Step 2. Go to P/S System Check and recheck DTC. P/S control module power supply and ground circuit check Check P/S control module power supply and ground circuits. Is check result OK? Go to Step 3. Repair or replace defective wire harness. P/S control module power supply voltage check Measure battery voltage with engine running at 3,000 rpm. Is voltage between 9 V and 15 V? Substitute a known-good steering column assembly and recheck DTC. Check charging system. K14C model: Generator Test:K14C K14D model: Generator Test:K14D

%sdatacharaents; ]> DTC B1032-00 / B1033-00 / B1034-00 / B1035-00 / B1036-00 / B1037-00 / B1039-00 DTC Detecting Condition and Trouble Area DTC detecting condition Trouble area B1032-00: Front Air Bag Deployment Record Deployment of front air bag has been recorded in SDM. If this DTC has been recorded even though air bag or pretensioner has not been deployed SDM Driver and/or passenger air bag (inflator) module Driver side-air bag (inflator) module Passenger side-air bag (inflator) module Driver and/or passenger front seat belt pretensioner Driver curtain air bag (inflator) module Passenger curtain air bag (inflator) module Left-side and/or right-side rear seat belt pretensioner Those initiator circuits B1033-00: Side Air Bag Deployment Record (Driver Side) Deployment of driver side-air bag (inflator) module has been recorded in SDM. B1034-00: Side Air Bag Deployment Record (Passenger Side) Deployment of passenger side-air bag (inflator) module has been recorded in SDM. B1035-00: Seat Belt Pretensioner Activation Record Activation of front seat belt pretensioner has been recorded in SDM. B1036-00: Curtain Air Bag Deployment Record (Driver Side) Deployment of driver curtain air bag (inflator) module has been recorded in SDM. B1037-00: Curtain Air Bag Deployment Record (Passenger Side) Deployment of passenger curtain air bag (inflator) module has been recorded in SDM. B1039-00: Rear Seat Belt Pretensioner Activation Record Activation of rear seat belt pretensioner has been recorded in SDM. If DTC B1032-00, B1033-00, B1034-00, B1035-00, B1036-00, B1037-00 or B1039-00 is stored in SDM, it is not possible to clear DTC. DTC Troubleshooting Was “Air Bag Diagnostic System Check” performed? Replace SDM. Go to Air Bag Diagnostic System Check . Upon completion of inspection and repair work, perform the following items. Reconnect all air bag system components and check that all components are properly mounted. Repeat Air Bag Diagnostic System Check to check that the trouble has been solved.

DTC Troubleshooting This DTC might be detected when battery is run down or the voltage is very low. DTC check Check DTC in BCM. Is DTC U0100 detected? Go to troubleshooting in DTC U0100 . Go to Step 2. Parameter check Connect SUZUKI scan tool to DLC when ignition is “OFF”. Set ignition “ON”. Check “Brake Light Switch” displayed on SUZUKI scan tool. Is check result OK? End. (Intermittent trouble.) Go to Step 3. Brake light switch signal circuit check Disconnect negative (–) cable at battery. Disconnect ECM and “E323” connectors. Check for proper terminal connection to ECM and “E323” connectors. If connections are OK, check that resistance of “A1” circuit is less than 1 Ω. Is check result OK? Go to Step 4. Repair or replace defective wire harness. Brake light switch signal circuit check Disconnect brake light switch connector. Connect “E323” connector. Check that resistance between “A2” circuit terminal at brake light switch connector and “A1” circuit terminal at ECM connector is less than 1 Ω. Is check result OK? Replace ECM and recheck DTC. Go to Step 5. Brake light switch check Check brake light switch. Is check result OK? Go to Step 6. Replace brake light switch. DTC check Connect brake light switch and ECM connectors. Connect negative (–) cable at battery. Perform DTC Confirmation Procedure and then recheck DTC. Is current DTC B1137 detected? Replace BCM and recheck DTC. End. (Intermittent trouble.)

## AENLSB0AA504022 - DTC B1161 / B1163
Path: ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0AA504022.xml
Scopes: bookmarks: 10 - Control Systems > 10E - Keyless Start System > Diagnostic Information and Procedures || dtc: 10 - Control Systems > Keyless Start System > Diagnostic Information and Procedures
Top outgoing references:
- AENLSB0AA504022002 -> unresolved | DTC Confirmation Procedure
- AENLSB0AA504022002 -> unresolved | DTC Confirmation Procedure
Excerpt:
%sdatacharaents; ]> DTC B1161 / B1163 DTC Detecting Condition and Trouble Area DTC detecting condition Trouble area B1161: Steering Lock Unit Information Mismatched Verification error between BCM and steering lock unit. Steering lock unit circuit / connector Steering lock unit BCM B1163: Steering Lock Unit Unregistered No registration of steering lock unit. BCM and steering lock unit check registration data each other. Therefore, if ignition cannot be set “ON”, it is necessary to replace steering lock unit with new one. DTC Confirmation Procedure Repeat lock / unlock operation of steering lock unit several times. Lock: Change ignition “ON” to “OFF” and open a door. Unlock: Set ignition “ON” at steering lock conditions. DTC Troubleshooting DTC check Clear DTC. Set ignition “OFF”. Perform DTC Confirmation Procedure and then recheck DTC. Is DTC B1161 or B1163 still detected? Go to Step 2. End. (Intermittent trouble.) DTC check Replace steering lock unit. Perform DTC Confirmation Procedure and then recheck DTC. Is DTC B1161 or B1163 still detected? Replace BCM and recheck DTC. End. (Steering lock unit was faulty)

- AENLSB0AA812001 -> ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0AA812001.xml | CAN Communication System Circuit Diagram:CAN (K14C Model)
- AENLSB0AAA14019018 -> unresolved | Diagnosis flow: [d]
Excerpt:
%sdatacharaents; ]> DTC C16C0 DTC Detecting Condition and Trouble Area DTC detecting condition Trouble area C16C0: Control Temporary Inhibition This DTC is detected if any one of the following conditions is met. Power supply voltage to dual sensor brake support control module is higher than specified value. Power supply voltage to dual sensor brake support control module is lower than specified value. Poor visibility of camera. Poor visibility of laser radar. Internal temperature of dual sensor brake support control module is higher than specified value. Received abnormal condition in control data from lighting and turn signal switch through CAN communication temporarily. Received abnormal condition in control data from ESP ® control module through CAN communication temporarily. Dual sensor brake support control module power supply / ground circuit Charging system Lighting and turn signal switch Windshield ESP ® control module Dual sensor brake support control module Circuit Diagram [A]: Dual sensor brake support control module connector (View: [a]) 1. Dual sensor brake support control module 4. “IG1 SIG3” fuse A1: Dual sensor brake support control module power supply circuit 2. CAN driver 5. Each control module K14C model: CAN Communication System Circuit Diagram:CAN (K14C Model) K14D model: CAN Communication System Circuit Diagram:CAN (K14D Model) B1: Dual sensor brake support control module ground circuit 3. IG11 relay System Description When dual sensor brake support control module judges that the system impairs the original performance, dual sensor brake support control module suspends each control, and outputs DTC C16C0. However, when the cause of the suspension is eliminated, dual sensor brake support control module turns OFF dual sensor brake support indicator light, clears the DTC and resumes the system function automatically. DTC Confirmation Procedure Set ignition “ON”. DTC Troubleshooting DTC C16C0 can be detected as a current DTC but it is not stored as a history DTC. The factor of the malfunction can be checked in “Data List” of SUZUKI scan tool. (except parameters related to steering angle sensor and ESP ® system) Check each parameter in the table

## AENLSB0A0000019 - Warning for Dual Sensor Brake Support System
Path: ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A0000019.xml
Scopes: bookmarks: 00 - Precautions > 00 - Precautions > Precautions
Top outgoing references:
- AENLSB0AAA16002 -> ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0AAA16002.xml | Dual Sensor Brake Support Control Module Aiming Adjustment:Dual Sensor Brake Support
Excerpt:
%sdatacharaents; ]> Warning for Dual Sensor Brake Support System Failure to take the following precautions may cause a serious accident affected by malfunction of dual sensor brake support system. Do not affix sticker including clear film or paint on windshield. Do not drop or otherwise expose dual sensor brake support control module to large shock. The dual sensor brake support control module that has received large shock should be replaced. When replacing windshield and windshield wiper blade, use SUZUKI genuine parts. After removing / installing or replacing windshield, perform Dual Sensor Brake Support Control Module Aiming Adjustment:Dual Sensor Brake Support . Do not modify suspension. Modification of the vehicle’s height or inclination may affect correct detection of a running vehicle ahead. Use tires of the same size and brand. Do not mix tires of different wear. Driving a vehicle which might have been exposed to impact at around vehicle front i.e. the vehicle involved in an accident, automatic brake could work suddenly and could cause a serious accident. When driving such a vehicle, disable dual sensor brake support system by pushing the dual sensor brake support OFF switch or ESP ® OFF switch for a long time to turn on dual sensor brake support OFF light beforehand. When vehicle is hoisted and wheels are rotated with engine running, disable dual sensor brake support system beforehand. If not, dual sensor brake support could work unexpectedly.

---

(3 D/C detection logic) OCV and/or its circuit ECM P2088: “A” Camshaft Position Actuator Control Circuit Low (Bank1) OCV drive circuit is shorted to ground. (3 D/C detection logic) P2089: “A” Camshaft Position Actuator Control Circuit High (Bank1) OCV drive circuit is shorted to power supply. (3 D/C detection logic) Circuit Diagram [A]: ECM connector (View: [a]) A2: OCV drive circuit 2. ECM A1: OCV power supply circuit 1. OCV 3. To main relay DTC Confirmation Procedure If you do not select a suitable route and take proper precautions when performing a road test, an accident can occur. Select a level road with no traffic to minimize the risk of accident. Perform the test extremely carefully. Perform the road test with 2 persons, a driver and a tester. Limit the driving speed permissible in road conditions and regulations. Check that the following condition is met in this “DTC Confirmation Procedure”. 8.5 V < battery voltage < 16.5 V Warm up engine and repeat acceleration and deceleration between 30 and 80 km/h (19 and 50 mile/h) more than 10 times. DTC Troubleshooting Was “Engine and Emission Control System Check” performed? Go to Step 2. Go to Engine and Emission Control System Check:K14C . OCV power supply circuit check Set ignition “OFF”, disconnect connector from OCV. Check for proper terminal connection to OCV connector. If connection is OK, set ignition “ON”. Check that voltage between “A1” and ground is battery voltage. Is check result OK? Go to Step 3. Repair or replace defective wire harness. OCV drive circuit check Set ignition “OFF”, disconnect “C01” and “E01” connectors from ECM. Check for proper terminal connections to “C01” and “E01” connectors. If connections are OK, check the following points. Resistance of “A2” circuit: less than 1 Ω Resistance between “A2” circuit and ground: infinity Resistance between “A1” circuit terminal and “A2” circuit terminal at OCV connector: infinity Voltage of “A2” circuit: approx. 0 V (When ignition is “ON”)

Top outgoing references:
- AENLSB0A1114001 -> ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A1114001.xml | Engine and Emission Control System Check:K14C
Excerpt:
%sdatacharaents; ]> DTC P0087 / P0088 DTC Detecting Condition and Trouble Area DTC detecting condition Trouble area P0087: Fuel Rail / System Pressure – Too Low Measured fuel rail pressure is far below targeted fuel rail pressure for specified time. (1 D/C detection logic) Fuel pressure sensor Low fuel pressure circuit (fuel pressure regulator in fuel pump, fuel pump and/or its circuit, fuel filter, fuel hose, fuel pipe) High fuel pressure circuit (high pressure fuel pump, fuel rail, high pressure pipe, fuel injector) ECM P0088: Fuel Rail / System Pressure – Too High Measured fuel rail pressure is far above targeted fuel rail pressure for specified time. (1 D/C detection logic) DTC Confirmation Procedure If you do not select a suitable route and take proper precautions when performing a road test, an accident can occur. Select a level road with no traffic to minimize the risk of accident. Perform the test extremely carefully. Perform the road test with 2 persons, a driver and a tester. Limit the driving speed permissible in road conditions and regulations. Check that the following condition is met in this “DTC Confirmation Procedure”. Battery voltage < 16 V Check that the following DTCs are not detected in this “DTC Confirmation Procedure”. Fuel pressure sensor (P0192 and P0193) Warm up engine to normal operating temperature. Drive vehicle at 50 to 80 km/h (31 to 50 mile/h) constantly for 5 min. or more. DTC Troubleshooting Was “Engine and Emission Control System Check” performed? Go to Step 2. Go to Engine and Emission Control System Check:K14C . Low fuel pressure circuit check Check low fuel pressure circuit. Is check result OK? Go to Step 3. Repair or replace defective part(s). High fuel pressure circuit check Check high fuel pressure circuit. Is check result OK? Replace ECM and recheck DTC. Repair or replace defective part(s).

(3 D/C detection logic) EVAP canister purge valve and/or its circuit ECM P0458: Evaporative Emission System Purge Control Valve Circuit Low EVAP canister purge valve drive circuit is shorted to ground with engine running. EVAP canister purge valve 3. ECM DTC Confirmation Procedure Check that the following condition is met in this “DTC Confirmation Procedure”. 8.5 V < battery voltage < 16 V Warm up engine to normal operating temperature. Run engine at idle speed for 1 min. DTC Troubleshooting Was “Engine and Emission Control System Check” performed? Go to Step 2. Go to Engine and Emission Control System Check:K14C . EVAP canister purge valve power supply circuit check Set ignition “OFF”, disconnect EVAP canister purge valve connector. Check for proper terminal connection to EVAP canister purge valve connector. If connection is OK, set ignition “ON”. Check that voltage between “A1” and ground is battery voltage. Is check result OK? Go to Step 3. Repair or replace defective wire harness. EVAP canister purge valve drive circuit check Set ignition “OFF”, disconnect “C01” and “E01” connectors from ECM. Check for proper terminal connection to “C01” and “E01” connectors. If connections are OK, check the following points. Resistance of “B1” circuit: less than 1 Ω Resistance between “B1” circuit and ground: infinity Resistance between “A1” circuit terminal and “B1” circuit terminal at EVAP canister purge valve connector: infinity Voltage of “B1” circuit: approx. 0 V (When ignition is “ON”) Is check result OK? Excerpt:
%sdatacharaents; ]> DTC P0500 DTC Detecting Condition and Trouble Area DTC detecting condition Trouble area P0500: Vehicle Speed Sensor “A” Any of the following condition is met. Vehicle speed is slower than 4 km/h (2 mile/h) for 12 sec. at fuel cut condition. Vehicle speed is not inputted by CAN communication. (3 D/C detection logic) CAN communication line Wheel speed sensor ESP ® control module ECM Circuit Diagram [A]: ECM connector (View: [a]) 3. Left-rear wheel speed sensor 6. ECM 1. Left-front wheel speed sensor 4. Right-rear wheel speed sensor 7. CAN driver 2. Right-front wheel speed sensor 5. ESP ® control module DTC Confirmation Procedure If you do not select a suitable route and take proper precautions when performing a road test, an accident can occur. Select a level road with no traffic to minimize the risk of accident. Perform the test extremely carefully. Perform the road test with 2 persons, a driver and a tester. Limit the driving speed permissible in road conditions and regulations. Warm up engine to normal operating temperature. With engine speed at 1,000 to 4,250 rpm drive vehicle at 60 km/h (37 mile/h) constantly for 1 min. or more at 5th gear (M/T model) or D range (A/T model). (Keep throttle valve opening constant in this step.) Release accelerator pedal, and keep vehicle coasting for 10 sec. in engine braking condition. (Fuel cut) DTC Troubleshooting Was “Engine and Emission Control System Check” performed? Go to Step 2. Go to Engine and Emission Control System Check:K14C . DTC check Is there any CAN-DTC detected? Go to troubleshooting for CAN-DTC. Go to Step 3. DTC check Check DTC in ESP ® control module. Is there any DTC(s) detected? Go to troubleshooting for applicable DTC. Replace ECM and recheck DTC.

Set A/C switch ON for 10 sec. Set A/C switch OFF. DTC Troubleshooting Was “Engine and Emission Control System Check” performed? Go to Step 2. Go to Engine and Emission Control System Check:K14C . If connection is OK, set ignition “ON”. Check that voltage between “A1” and ground is battery voltage. Is check result OK? Go to Step 3. Repair or replace defective wire harness. A/C compressor relay check Check A/C compressor relay. Is check result OK? Go to Step 4. Replace A/C compressor relay. A/C compressor relay drive circuit check Set ignition “OFF”, disconnect “C01” and “E01” connectors from ECM. Check for proper terminal connection to “C01” and “E01” connectors. If connections are OK, check the following points. (1 D/C detection logic) Throttle actuator and/or its circuit Electric throttle body assembly ECM Circuit Diagram [A]: ECM connector (View: [a]) B3: TP sensor (sub) signal circuit 4. (1 D/C detection logic) Barometric pressure sensor in ECM P2227: Barometric Pressure Circuit Range / Performance Any of the following conditions is met. ## AENLSB0A1416023 - Cylinder Head Cover Removal and Installation
Path: ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A1416023.xml
Scopes: bookmarks: 1 - Engine > 1D - Engine Mechanical > Repair Instructions > K14C
Top outgoing references:
- AENLSB0A1416022 -> ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A1416022.xml | Cylinder Head Cover Components:K14C
Excerpt:
%sdatacharaents; ]> Cylinder Head Cover Removal and Installation Cylinder Head Cover Components:K14C Removal Disconnect negative (–) cable at battery. Remove air cleaner assembly and air cleaner outlet hose No.1. Remove high pressure fuel pump cover. Remove ignition coil assemblies. Disconnect engine harness clamps (1). Remove engine harness bracket bolts (2). Remove engine cover bracket No.1 (3). Remove oil level gauge (4). Disconnect breather hose (5) from cylinder head cover. Disconnect air bypass valve connector (6) and air bypass valve control hose No.3 (7). Loosen cylinder head cover bolts No.1 (1) and No.2 (2) in numerical order (“1” – “14”) evenly and gradually. Remove cylinder head cover with cylinder head cover gasket. Installation Remove oil, old sealant and dust from sealing surfaces on cylinder head and cover. Install new gasket (2) to cylinder head cover (1). Apply sealant “A” to the mating surface as shown. “A” Sealant 99000–31250 SUZUKI Bond No.1207F Sealant width on sealing surface “a”: 4.0 mm (0.16 in.) Install cylinder head cover to cylinder head. When installing cylinder head cover, use care so that cylinder head cover gasket will not get out of place or fall off. Tighten cylinder head cover bolts No.1 and No.2 according to the following procedure. Temporarily tighten cylinder head cover bolts No.1 (1) to position the cylinder head cover. Tighten cylinder head cover bolts No.1 and No.2 to 5.0 N·m (0.51 kgf-m, 4.0 lbf-ft) in numerical order (“1” – “14”) evenly and gradually. Retighten cylinder head cover bolts No.2 to 10 N·m (1.0 kgf-m, 7.5 lbf-ft) and cylinder head cover bolts No.1 to 7.5 N·m (0.76 kgf-m, 5.5 lbf-ft) in the same manner as in Step b). Cylinder head cover bolt No.1* a 5.0 N·m → 7.5 N·m (0.51 kgf-m → 0.76 kgf-m, 4.0 lbf-ft → 5.5 lbf-ft) Cylinder head cover bolt No.2* b 5.0 N·m → 10 N·m (0.51 kgf-m → 1.0 kgf-m, 4.0 lbf-ft → 7.5 lbf-ft) Connect air bypass valve control hose No.3 (7) and air bypass valve connector (6). Connect breather hose (5) to cylinder head cover. Install oil level gauge (4). Install engine cover bracket No.1 (3). Tighten engine harness bracket bolts (2). Connect engine harness clamps (1). Install ignition coil

## AENLSB0A1416030 - Flywheel / Drive Plate Removal and Installation
Path: ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A1416030.xml
Scopes: bookmarks: 1 - Engine > 1D - Engine Mechanical > Repair Instructions > K14C
Top outgoing references:
- AENLSB0A1416031 -> ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A1416031.xml | Flywheel / Drive Plate Inspection:K14C
Excerpt:
%sdatacharaents; ]> Flywheel / Drive Plate Removal and Installation Removal For M/T model, remove clutch cover and clutch disc. For A/T model, remove transaxle assembly. Lock flywheel (M/T model) or drive plate (A/T model) using special tool as shown in figure. (A) 09924–17811 Remove flywheel (M/T model) or drive plate (A/T model) from crankshaft. For A/T model, separate drive plate flange and drive rear plate from drive plate. Installation Flywheel / Drive Plate Inspection:K14C Install flywheel (M/T model) or drive plate (A/T model) according to the following procedure. The flywheel bolts (M/T model) or drive plate bolts (A/T model) are pre-coated with adhesive. If these bolts are reused, they may work loose. Replace the flywheel bolts (M/T model) or drive plate bolts (A/T model) with new ones whenever they are removed. For M/T model, install new flywheel bolts. For A/T model, install drive plate flange, drive plate and drive rear plate using new drive plate bolts. Lock flywheel (M/T model) or drive plate (A/T model) using special tool as shown in figure. (A) 09924–17811 For M/T model, tighten all flywheel bolts evenly and gradually to 20 N·m (2.0 kgf-m, 15.0 lbf-ft). For M/T model, retighten them 34°. Flywheel bolt* a 20 N·m → +34° (2.0 kgf-m → +34°, 15.0 lbf-ft → +34°) For A/T model, tighten drive plate bolts to specified torque. Drive plate bolt a 75 7.6 55.5 For M/T model, install clutch cover and clutch disc. For A/T model, install transmission assembly.

Excerpt:
%sdatacharaents; ]> High Pressure Pipe Removal and Installation Before performing the following procedure, observe Precautions for Fuel System Service:K14C . Removal Hoist vehicle and remove engine undercover and right side engine under side cover. Relieve fuel pressure. Remove electric throttle body assembly. Remove high pressure fuel pump cover. Remove high pressure pipe (1). Installation Failure to take proper precautions when installing the high pressure pipe can cause damage to the high pressure pipe and may lead to fuel leakage. Take the following precautions when removing and installing the high pressure pipe. Never reuse high pressure pipe. When the union nut is tightened using extension (claw foot) until the torque wrench reads the specified torque, the nut will actually be tightened to a torque much more than the specified torque. This could damage the thread. Use the formula shown below to determine the correct reading of the torque wrench for its length. Then tighten the union nut of high pressure pipe until the torque wrench indicates the value thus determined. Tightening torque formula M = T x L / (L + “a”) M: Value indicated on torque wrench T: Specified tightening torque L: Effective length of torque wrench “a”: Effective length of extension “b”: Handle center line (power point) “c”: Pivot point (fulcrum) Install new high pressure pipe according to the following procedure. Check that notch (1) on crankshaft pulley is positioned within range “b”. “a”: 140° “b”: 20° Loosen high pressure fuel pump bolts (1), tighten them to 5.0 N·m (0.51 kgf-m, 4.0 lbf-ft) and then loosen 90 – 120°. Tighten high pressure pipe union nuts (2) by hand until seated and temporarily tighten high pressure pipe bolt (3). Tighten high pressure pipe union nuts (1) to specified torque in numerical order (“1” – “2”) as shown in figure. High pressure pipe union nut* a 33 3.4 24.5 Tighten high pressure fuel pump bolts (2) to specified torque. High pressure fuel pump bolt* b 13 1.3 9.5 Tighten high pressure pipe bolt (3) to specified torque. High pressure pipe bolt* c 11 1.1 8.5 Install electric throttle body assembly. With engine stopped, set ignition “ON” and check for fuel leak

## AENLSB0A1716010 - High Pressure Fuel Pump Removal and Installation
Path: ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A1716010.xml
Scopes: bookmarks: 1 - Engine > 1G - Fuel System > Repair Instructions > K14C
Top outgoing references:
- AENLSB0A1710001 -> ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A1710001.xml | Precautions for Fuel System Service:K14C
Excerpt:
%sdatacharaents; ]> High Pressure Fuel Pump Removal and Installation Before performing the following procedure, observe Precautions for Fuel System Service:K14C . Removal Relieve fuel pressure. Remove high pressure pipe. Disconnect high pressure fuel pump connector. Disconnect fuel feed hose (1) from high pressure fuel pump. Remove high pressure fuel pump (2) with high pressure fuel pump O-ring and fuel pump tappet (3). Installation Clean mating surfaces of high pressure fuel pump and camshaft housing. Turn crankshaft so that notch (1) on crankshaft pulley is positioned within range “b” and check that base circle (heel) of high pressure fuel pump cam is aligned with center line of high pressure fuel pump. “a”: 140° “b”: 20° Install fuel pump tappet noting the following points. Apply engine oil to roller and periphery of tappet. Insert tappet (1) aligning protrusion (2) with groove (3) in camshaft rear housing hole. Apply engine oil to new O-ring and then fit it to high pressure fuel pump. Install high pressure fuel pump (1) and tighten high pressure fuel pump bolts (2) as follows. Tighten high pressure fuel pump bolts evenly until the pump is seated on the housing. Tighten high pressure fuel pump bolts to 5.0 N·m (0.51 kgf-m, 4.0 lbf-ft). Connect fuel feed hose to high pressure fuel pump as it was. Connect high pressure fuel pump connector. Install new high pressure pipe and tighten high pressure fuel pump bolts to specified torque. With engine stopped, set ignition “ON” and check for fuel leakage.

## AENLSB0A5104022 - DTC P0562 / P0563
Path: ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A5104022.xml
Scopes: bookmarks: 5 - Transmission / Transaxle > 5A - Automatic Transmission/Transaxle > Diagnostic Information and Procedures || dtc: 5 - Transmission / Transaxle > Automatic Transmission/Transaxle > Diagnostic Information and Procedures
Top outgoing references:
- AENLSB0A5104001 -> ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A5104001.xml | A/T Control System Check
Excerpt:
%sdatacharaents; ]> DTC P0562 / P0563 DTC Detecting Condition and Trouble Area DTC detecting condition Trouble area P0562: System Voltage Low Main power supply circuit voltage is lower than specified value for 20 sec. (1 D/C detection logic) TCM power supply circuit and/or ground circuit Charging system TCM P0563: System Voltage High Main power supply circuit voltage is higher than specified value for 60 sec. (1 D/C detection logic) Circuit Diagram [A]: TCM connector (View: [a]) 1. TCM 5. “IGN” fuse A1: Main power supply circuit 2. “T/M” fuse 6. IG11 relay A2: Ignition power supply circuit 3. “IG1 SIG” fuse 7. J/B B1: TCM ground circuit 4. Main fuse box 8. To BCM DTC Confirmation Procedure Check that the following condition is met in this “DTC Confirmation Procedure”. DTC U0073 or U0100 is not detected. Start engine at idle for 3 minute. DTC Troubleshooting Was “A/T Control System Check” performed? Go to Step 2. Go to A/T Control System Check . TCM power supply circuit and ground circuit check Check TCM power supply and ground circuits. Is check result OK? Go to Step 3. Repair or replace defective wire harness. TCM power supply voltage check Check power supply voltage under the following conditions. Ignition “ON” Engine running at 3,000 rpm Is voltage 10 to 15 V? Substitute a known-good TCM and recheck DTC. Check charging system.

## AENLSB0A5104024 - DTC P0604 / P062F / P1702 / P184C
Path: ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A5104024.xml
Scopes: bookmarks: 5 - Transmission / Transaxle > 5A - Automatic Transmission/Transaxle > Diagnostic Information and Procedures || dtc: 5 - Transmission / Transaxle > Automatic Transmission/Transaxle > Diagnostic Information and Procedures
Top outgoing references:
- AENLSB0A5104001 -> ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A5104001.xml | A/T Control System Check
Excerpt:
%sdatacharaents; ]> DTC P0604 / P062F / P1702 / P184C DTC Detecting Condition and Trouble Area DTC detecting condition Trouble area P0604: Internal Control Module Random Access Memory (RAM) Error TCM internal failure (RAM error). (1 D/C detection logic) TCM P062F: Internal Control Module EEPROM Error TCM internal failure (EEPROM error). (1 D/C detection logic) P1702: Internal Control Module Memory Check Sum Error TCM internal failure (memory error). (1 D/C detection logic) P184C: Solenoid Driver “A” circuit Range / Performance TCM internal failure (solenoid valve IC error). (1 D/C detection logic) DTC Confirmation Procedure P0604 / P062F / P1702 Set ignition “ON” for 10 sec. P184C Check that the following condition is met in this “DTC Confirmation Procedure”. DTC U0073 or U0100 is not detected. Set ignition “ON” for 10 sec. DTC Troubleshooting Was “A/T Control System Check” performed? Go to Step 2. Go to A/T Control System Check . DTC check Is there any DTC(s) other than P0604, P062F, P1702 or P184C? Go to troubleshooting for applicable DTC. Go to Step 3. DTC check Clear DTC. Set ignition “OFF”. Perform “DTC Confirmation Procedure”, and check DTC. Is DTC P0604, P062F, P1702 or P184C still indicated? Substitute a known-good TCM and recheck DTC. Check for intermittent trouble.

## AENLSB0A5104025 - DTC P0706 / P0707 / P0708
Path: ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A5104025.xml
Scopes: bookmarks: 5 - Transmission / Transaxle > 5A - Automatic Transmission/Transaxle > Diagnostic Information and Procedures || dtc: 5 - Transmission / Transaxle > Automatic Transmission/Transaxle > Diagnostic Information and Procedures
Top outgoing references:
- AENLSB0A5104001 -> ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A5104001.xml | A/T Control System Check
Excerpt:
%sdatacharaents; ]> DTC P0706 / P0707 / P0708 DTC Detecting Condition and Trouble Area DTC detecting condition Trouble area P0706: Transmission Range Sensor “A” Circuit Range / Performance Faulty information is received from transmission range sensor. (Transmission range sensor is stuck.) (1 D/C detection logic) TCM P0707: Transmission Range Sensor “A” Circuit Low Transmission range sensor signal circuit voltage is lower than specified value. (1 D/C detection logic) P0708: Transmission Range Sensor “A” Circuit High Transmission range sensor signal circuit voltage is higher than specified value. (1 D/C detection logic) DTC Confirmation Procedure Check that the following condition is met in this “DTC Confirmation Procedure”. DTC U0073 or U0100 is not detected. Set ignition “ON”. Shift select lever from P to M range. DTC Troubleshooting Was “A/T Control System Check” performed? Go to Step 2. Go to A/T Control System Check . DTC check Clear DTC. Set ignition “OFF”. Perform “DTC Confirmation Procedure”, and check DTC. Is DTC P0706, P0707 or P0708 still indicated? Substitute a known-good TCM and recheck DTC. Check for intermittent trouble.

## AENLSB0A5104026 - DTC P0711 / P0712 / P0713
Path: ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A5104026.xml
Scopes: bookmarks: 5 - Transmission / Transaxle > 5A - Automatic Transmission/Transaxle > Diagnostic Information and Procedures || dtc: 5 - Transmission / Transaxle > Automatic Transmission/Transaxle > Diagnostic Information and Procedures
Top outgoing references:
- AENLSB0A5104001 -> ./source-mirror/SWIFT_A2L310_A2L412_A2L414/xml/AENLSB0A/AENLSB0A5104001.xml | A/T Control System Check
Excerpt:
%sdatacharaents; ]> DTC P0711 / P0712 / P0713 DTC Detecting Condition and Trouble Area DTC detecting condition Trouble area P0711: Transmission Fluid Temperature Sensor “A” Circuit Range / Performance ATF temperature sensor signal circuit voltage is unchanged for 10 minutes during driving. (2 D/C detection logic) Solenoid harness with ATF temperature sensor TCM P0712: Transmission Fluid Temperature Sensor “A” Circuit Low ATF temperature sensor signal circuit voltage is lower than specified value for 5 sec. (2 D/C detection logic) P0713: Transmission Fluid Temperature Sensor “A” Circuit High ATF temperature sensor signal circuit voltage is higher than specified value for 5 sec. after driving. (2 D/C detection logic) DTC Confirmation Procedure P0711 / P0713 If you do not select a suitable route and take proper precautions when performing a road test, an accident can occur. Select a level road with no traffic to minimize the risk of accident. Perform the test extremely carefully. Perform the road test with 2 persons, a driver and a tester. Limit the driving speed permissible in road conditions and regulations. Warm up engine to normal operating temperature. Drive vehicle for 10 minutes or more. P0712 Set ignition “ON” for 10 sec. DTC Troubleshooting Was “A/T Control System Check” performed? Go to Step 2. Go to A/T Control System Check . ATF temperature sensor check Check ATF temperature sensor. Is check result OK? Substitute a known-good TCM and recheck DTC. Replace solenoid harness with ATF temperature sensor.

(1 D/C detection logic) Input shaft speed sensor Solenoid harness with ATF temperature sensor TCM P1738: Input / Turbine Speed Sensor “A” Circuit Low Input shaft speed sensor signal circuit is open or shorted to ground. (1 D/C detection logic) P1739: Input / Turbine Speed Sensor “A” Circuit High Input shaft speed sensor signal circuit is shorted to power supply. (1 D/C detection logic) Circuit Diagram [A]: Solenoid harness connector (View: [a]) A2: Input shaft speed sensor (–) circuit 3. A/T [B]: Input shaft speed sensor connector (View: [a]) 1. TCM 4. Input shaft speed sensor A1: Input shaft speed sensor (+) circuit 2. CPU DTC Confirmation Procedure P0717 If you do not select a suitable route and take proper precautions when performing a road test, an accident can occur. Select a level road with no traffic to minimize the risk of accident. Perform the test extremely carefully. Perform the road test with 2 persons, a driver and a tester. Limit the driving speed permissible in road conditions and regulations. Check that the following condition is met in this “DTC Confirmation Procedure”. DTC U0073 or U0100 is not detected. Warm up engine to normal operating temperature. Drive vehicle at approx. 30 km/h (19 mile/h) for a total of 5 minutes. P1738 / P1739 Set ignition “ON” for 10 sec. DTC Troubleshooting Was “A/T Control System Check” performed? Go to Step 2. Go to A/T Control System Check . Scan tool parameter check Warm up engine to normal operating temperature. Drive vehicle. Check “Engine Speed” and “Input Shaft Revolution” using “Data list” mode on SUZUKI scan tool. Check that “Input Shaft Revolution” varies depending on “Engine Speed”. Is check result OK? Check for intermittent trouble. Go to Step 3. Solenoid harness check Remove TCM. Remove valve body assembly. Disconnect input shaft speed sensor connector. Check for proper terminal connections to solenoid harness connector and input s
